          <p>
            As of 2014<sup
              class="plainlinks noprint asof-tag update"
              style="display: none"
              ><a
                class="external text"
                href="//en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ebola_virus_epidemic_in_West_Africa&amp;action=edit"
                >[update]</a
              ></sup
            >, an <a href="/wiki/Epidemic" title="Epidemic">epidemic</a> of
            <a href="/wiki/Ebola_virus_disease" title="Ebola virus disease"
              >Ebola virus disease</a
            >
            (EVD) is ongoing in
            <a href="/wiki/West_Africa" title="West Africa">West Africa</a>. The
            <a href="/wiki/Outbreak" title="Outbreak">outbreak</a> began in
            <a href="/wiki/Guinea" title="Guinea">Guinea</a> in December 2013,
            but was not detected until March 2014,<sup
              id="cite_ref-CRM2014_3-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-CRM2014-3"
                ><span>[</span>3<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            after which it spread to
            <a href="/wiki/Liberia" title="Liberia">Liberia</a>,
            <a href="/wiki/Sierra_Leone" title="Sierra Leone">Sierra Leone</a>,
            <a href="/wiki/Nigeria" title="Nigeria">Nigeria</a> and
            <a href="/wiki/Senegal" title="Senegal">Senegal</a>. The outbreak is
            caused by
            <i
              ><a href="/wiki/Ebola_virus" title="Ebola virus"
                >Ebola virus</a
              ></i
            >
            (EBOV). It is the most severe
            <a
              href="/wiki/List_of_Ebola_outbreaks"
              title="List of Ebola outbreaks"
              >outbreak of Ebola</a
            >
            in terms of the number of human cases and deaths since the discovery
            of the virus in 1976,<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-4"><span>[</span>4<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            with the number of cases from the current outbreak now outnumbering
            the combined cases from all known previous outbreaks.<sup
              id="cite_ref-5"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-5"><span>[</span>5<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Another outbreak in the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo"
              title="Democratic Republic of the Congo"
              >Democratic Republic of the Congo</a
            >, which has 53 possible and confirmed cases and 31 deaths as of 2
            September 2014, is believed to be unrelated to the West African
            outbreak.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-6"><span>[</span>6<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            As of 5 September 2014<sup
              class="plainlinks noprint asof-tag update"
              style="display: none"
              ><a
                class="external text"
                href="//en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ebola_virus_epidemic_in_West_Africa&amp;action=edit"
                >[update]</a
              ></sup
            >, the
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization"
              title="World Health Organization"
              >World Health Organization</a
            >
            (WHO) and the Centers for Disease Control (CDC) reported a total of
            3,967 suspected cases and 2,105 deaths (2,383 cases and 1,243 deaths
            being
            <a href="/wiki/Laboratory" title="Laboratory">laboratory</a>
            confirmed).<sup
              id="cite_ref-Ebola_Outbreak_total_7-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Ebola_Outbreak_total-7"
                ><span>[</span>7<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            Many experts believe that the official numbers substantially
            understate the size of the outbreak because of families' widespread
            reluctance to report cases.<sup
              id="cite_ref-cordon_8-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-cordon-8"
                ><span>[</span>8<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 28 August, the WHO reported an overall case fatality rate
            estimate of 52%, considerably lower than an average of rates
            reported from previous outbreaks.
          </p>
          <p>
            On 8 August, the outbreak was formally designated as a
            <a
              href="/wiki/Public_health_emergency_of_international_concern"
              title="Public health emergency of international concern"
              >public health emergency of international concern</a
            >.<sup id="cite_ref-WHO_PHEIC_8Aug2014_9-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO_PHEIC_8Aug2014-9"
                ><span>[</span>9<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            This is a legal designation used only twice before (for the
            <a href="/wiki/2009_flu_pandemic" title="2009 flu pandemic"
              >2009 H1N1 (swine flu) pandemic</a
            >
            and the 2014 resurgence of
            <a href="/wiki/Polio" title="Polio" class="mw-redirect">polio</a>)
            and invokes legal measures on disease prevention, surveillance,
            control, and response, by 194 signatory countries.<sup
              id="cite_ref-urlInside_Justice-WHO-PHEIC_10-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-urlInside_Justice-WHO-PHEIC-10"
                ><span>[</span>10<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 28 August, the WHO reported that it estimated that up to 20,000
            individuals could be infected before containment efforts bring the
            outbreak to a halt.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-11"><span>[</span>11<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            Affected countries have encountered many difficulties in their
            attempt to control the spread of Ebola. It is the first Ebola
            epidemic that West African nations have experienced and many
            individuals, including medical staff, lack knowledge and experience
            in dealing with a highly communicable disease. In some areas people
            have become suspicious of both the government and hospitals; some
            hospitals have been attacked by angry protestors who believe that
            the disease is a hoax or that the hospitals are responsible for the
            disease. Many of the areas that have been infected are areas of
            extreme poverty without even running water or soap to help control
            the spread of disease. Other factors include belief in – and
            reliance on – traditional
            <a href="/wiki/Folk_medicine" title="Folk medicine">folk remedies</a
            >,
            <a href="/wiki/Magical_thinking" title="Magical thinking"
              >magical beliefs</a
            >, and cultural practices that predispose to physical contact with
            the deceased, especially
            <a
              href="/wiki/Death_custom"
              title="Death custom"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >death customs</a
            >
            such as
            <a href="/wiki/Bathing_the_dead" title="Bathing the dead"
              >washing the body of the deceased</a
            >. Some hospitals lack basic supplies and are understaffed, which
            has increased the likelihood of staff catching the virus themselves.
            In August the WHO reported that ten percent of the dead have been
            health care workers.<sup
              class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact"
              style="white-space: nowrap"
              >[<i
                ><a
                  href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed"
                  title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"
                  ><span
                    title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2014)"
                    >citation needed</span
                  ></a
                ></i
              >]</sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            Various
            <a
              href="/wiki/Aid_organisation"
              title="Aid organisation"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >aid organisations</a
            >
            and international bodies, including the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States"
              title="Economic Community of West African States"
              >Economic Community of West African States</a
            >
            (ECOWAS), US
            <a
              href="/wiki/Centers_for_Disease_Control_and_Prevention"
              title="Centers for Disease Control and Prevention"
              >Centers for Disease Control and Prevention</a
            >
            (CDC), and the
            <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission"
              >European Commission</a
            >
            have donated funds and mobilised personnel to help counter the
            outbreak; charities including
            <a
              href="/wiki/M%C3%A9decins_Sans_Fronti%C3%A8res"
              title="Médecins Sans Frontières"
              >Médecins Sans Frontières</a
            >, the
            <a
              href="/wiki/International_Red_Cross_and_Red_Crescent_Movement"
              title="International Red Cross and Red Crescent Movement"
              >Red Cross</a
            >,<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-12"><span>[</span>12<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            and
            <a href="/wiki/Samaritan%27s_Purse" title="Samaritan's Purse"
              >Samaritan's Purse</a
            >
            are also working in the area. At the end of August the WHO reported
            that the loss of so many health workers was making it difficult for
            them to provide sufficient numbers of foreign medical staff, and the
            <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union"
              >African Union</a
            >
            launched an urgent initiative to recruit more health care workers
            from among its members.<sup id="cite_ref-WHO_13-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO-13"
                ><span>[</span>13<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <div class="toclimit-3">
