            Nigeria has seen twelve confirmed cases; four have died (including
            the index case) while another five, including two doctors and a
            nurse, have been declared disease-free and released.<sup
              id="cite_ref-2014_Ebola_Outbreak_in_West_Africa_128-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-2014_Ebola_Outbreak_in_West_Africa-128"
                ><span>[</span>128<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-129"><span>[</span>129<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Other than increased surveillance at the country’s borders, the
            Nigerian government states that they have also made attempts to
            control the spread of disease through an improvement in tracking,
            providing education to avert disinformation and increase accurate
            information, and the teaching of appropriate hygiene measures:
            "Efforts are currently ongoing to scale up and strengthen all
            aspects of response, including contact tracking, public information
            and community mobilization, case management and infection prevention
            and control, and coordination. There is now increased disease
            surveillance system in a bid to monitor, control, and prevent any
            occurrence of the disease".<sup
              id="cite_ref-2014_Ebola_Outbreak_in_West_Africa_128-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-2014_Ebola_Outbreak_in_West_Africa-128"
                ><span>[</span>128<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 22 August, a doctor who treated a Liberian diplomat in an unnamed
            hotel—who had contact with Patrick Sawyer—died in
            <a href="/wiki/Port_Harcourt" title="Port Harcourt"
              >Port Harcourt</a
            >
            from Ebola. The BBC report says the diplomat had escaped from
            quarantine in <a href="/wiki/Lagos" title="Lagos">Lagos</a> and
            travelled to the city for medical treatment. He however survived
            after being treated. At present, the total number of deaths from
            Ebola in Nigeria has been brought to six.<sup
              id="cite_ref-130"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-130"><span>[</span>130<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            The
            <a
              href="/wiki/Good_Heart_Specialist_Hospital"
              title="Good Heart Specialist Hospital"
              >Good Heart Hospital</a
            >
            and the unnamed hotel in
            <a href="/wiki/Rivers_State" title="Rivers State">Rivers State</a>
            have been shut down. As a result 70 suspected contacts have been
            quarantined.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-131"><span>[</span>131<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-132"><span>[</span>132<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Sierra_Leone">Sierra Leone</span>
          </h4>
          <p>
            The first person recorded to be infected with Ebola was a tribal
            healer who had treated an infected person, or persons, in her area
            and was reported to have died on 26 May. According to tribal
            tradition, her body was washed for her burial and several women from
            neighboring towns became infected.<sup
              id="cite_ref-Fox_News_133-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Fox_News-133"
                ><span>[</span>133<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 1 April, Sierra Leone instituted a temporary measure which
            includes reactivation of its "Active Surveillance Protocol" that
            will see all travellers into the country from either Guinea or
            Liberia subjected to strict screening to ascertain their state of
            health.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-134"><span>[</span>134<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            The government of Sierra Leone declared a state of emergency on 30
            July and deployed troops to quarantine the hot spots of the
            epidemic.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-135"><span>[</span>135<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 29 July, well-known physician Sheik Umar Khan, Sierra Leone's
            only expert on hemorrhagic fever, died after contacting Ebola at his
            clinic in Kenema. Khan had long worked with
            <a href="/wiki/Lassa_fever" title="Lassa fever">Lassa fever</a>
            patients, which kills over 5,000 a year in Sierra Leone, and he
            expanded his clinic to accept Ebola patients when the disease broke
            out. At his death, Sierra Leone President Ernest Bai Koroma
            celebrated Khan as a "national hero".<sup
              id="cite_ref-Fox_News_133-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Fox_News-133"
                ><span>[</span>133<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            In August, awareness campaigns in Freetown, Sierra Leone's capital,
            were delivered over the radio or through car loudspeakers.<sup
              id="cite_ref-136"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-136"><span>[</span>136<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Also in August, Sierra Leone passed a law that will subject a
            two-year jail term on anyone found to be hiding a person who is
            believed to be infected with Ebola disease. The new measure was
            announced as a top parliamentarian lashed out at neighbouring
            countries for failing to do more to curtail the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-137"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-137"><span>[</span>137<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 26 August, the WHO said it had shut down one of its two
            laboratories in Sierra Leone after a health worker there was
            infected with Ebola. The laboratory is situated in the
            <a href="/wiki/Kailahun" title="Kailahun">Kailahun</a> district, one
            of the worst affected areas in Sierra Leone. This may disrupt
            efforts to increase the global response to the outbreak of the
            disease in the district.<sup
              id="cite_ref-WHO_infected_SL_138-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO_infected_SL-138"
                ><span>[</span>138<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            "It's a temporary measure to take care of the welfare of our
            remaining workers," WHO spokesperson Christy Feig announced. He did
            not specify how long the closure would last, but they will return
            after the WHO assessment of the situation. The medical worker is one
            of the first WHO staff infected by the Ebola Virus. The worker was
            first treated at a government hospital in
            <a href="/wiki/Kenema" title="Kenema">Kenema</a> and then evacuated
            to Germany for further treatment.<sup
              id="cite_ref-WHO_infected_SL_138-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO_infected_SL-138"
                ><span>[</span>138<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-NBC_News_139-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-NBC_News-139"
                ><span>[</span>139<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Senegal">Senegal</span></h4>
          <p>
            In March, the
            <a href="/wiki/Senegal" title="Senegal">Senegal</a> Ministry of
            Interior ordered all movements of people through the southern border
            with Guinea to be suspended indefinitely to prevent the spread of
            the disease, according to a statement published on 29 March by state
            agency APS.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-140"><span>[</span>140<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 29 August, the Senegalese Health minister, Awa Marie Coll Seck,
            announced the first case of Ebola in the country. The patient
            arrived from the neighbouring country Guinea, where the virus was
            first reported. The case has been confirmed in Senegal.<sup
              id="cite_ref-2014_Ebola_Senegal_38-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-2014_Ebola_Senegal-38"
                ><span>[</span>38<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-2014_Ebola_Senegal_1_39-1" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-2014_Ebola_Senegal_1-39"
                ><span>[</span>39<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            The patient, a university student from Guinea, is being treated in
            <a href="/wiki/Dakar" title="Dakar">Dakar</a>. Samples were sent to
            the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Institut_Pasteur"
              title="Institut Pasteur"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Institut Pasteur</a
            >, where Ebola was confirmed. The
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization"
              title="World Health Organization"
              >World Health Organization</a
            >
            (WHO) was informed of the situation on 30 August
            <sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-141"><span>[</span>141<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            and is treating the situation "as a top priority emergency", and it
            has now dispatched operational personnel to Dakar.<sup
              id="cite_ref-142"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-142"><span>[</span>142<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span
              class="mw-headline"
              id="Countries_with_suspected_or_non-transmissive_cases"
              >Countries with suspected or non-transmissive cases</span
            >
          </h3>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Germany">Germany</span></h4>
          <p>
            <a href="/wiki/Germany" title="Germany">Germany</a> has set up a
            special isolation ward that will care for up to six patients at the
            <a
              href="/wiki/University_Medical_Center_Hamburg-Eppendorf"
              title="University Medical Center Hamburg-Eppendorf"
              >University Medical Center Hamburg-Eppendorf</a
            >
            to provide treatment for Ebola patients. On 27 August the first
            patient arrived, a
            <a href="/wiki/Senegalese" title="Senegalese" class="mw-redirect"
              >Senegalese</a
            >
            <a
              href="/wiki/Epidemiologist"
              title="Epidemiologist"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >epidemiologist</a
            >
            who was working for the World Health Organization in Sierra
            Leone.<sup id="cite_ref-NBC_News_139-1" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-NBC_News-139"
                ><span>[</span>139<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="India">India</span></h4>
          <p>
            On Tuesday 27 August a Health Ministry official said that 112 Indian
            citizens and four Nepalese citizens had landed in
            <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a> and
            <a href="/wiki/Delhi" title="Delhi">Delhi</a> from Liberia.<sup
              id="cite_ref-143"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-143"><span>[</span>143<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Of the 17 who had arrived in Delhi, one had fever symptoms and had
            been quarantined at the Airport Health Organisation (APHO), an
            airport medical facility. Six others were screened for Ebola, and
            five passengers who arrived on routine flights from affected
            countries showing fever symptoms had also been quarantined. Earlier,
            it was reported that an isolation facility with 120 beds was being
            created in the Hindu Hriday Samrat Jogeshwari trauma care hospital
            by
            <a
              href="/wiki/Brihanmumbai_Municipal_Corporation"
              title="Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation"
              >Brihanmumbai Municipal Corporation</a
            >
            (BMC).<sup
              id="cite_ref-Indian_suspected_cases_144-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Indian_suspected_cases-144"
                ><span>[</span>144<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 28 August, the Health Ministry reported that 821 people were
            being monitored and tracked for the Ebola virus.<sup
              id="cite_ref-145"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-145"><span>[</span>145<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Spain">Spain</span></h4>
          <p>
            On 5 August, the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Brothers_Hospitallers_of_St._John_of_God"
              title="Brothers Hospitallers of St. John of God"
              >Brothers Hospitallers of St. John of God</a
            >
            confirmed that the Spanish Brother Miguel Pajares was infected with
            the Ebola virus while volunteering in Liberia. His repatriation,
            coordinated by the Spanish Ministry of Defence, occurred on 6 August
            2014.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated3_146-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated3-146"
                ><span>[</span>146<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            Spanish authorities confirmed that the patient would be treated in
            the 'Carlos III' hospital in Madrid. The decision attracted some
            controversy, amid questions as to the authorities' ability to
            guarantee no risk of transmission.<sup
              id="cite_ref-147"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-147"><span>[</span>147<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Brother Pajares died from the virus on 12 August.<sup
              id="cite_ref-148"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-148"><span>[</span>148<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="United_Kingdom">United Kingdom</span>
          </h4>
          <p>
            In August, an isolation unit at the
            <a href="/wiki/Royal_Free_Hospital" title="Royal Free Hospital"
              >Royal Free Hospital</a
            >
            in
            <a href="/wiki/North_London" title="North London">North London</a>
            was prepared to treat patients with highly infectious diseases. On
            24 August, William Pooley, a British citizen, was medically
            evacuated from Sierra Leone for treatment in the newly created unit.
            Pooley, a British health worker, is the first British citizen
            confirmed to have contracted the disease in Sierra Leone.<sup
              id="cite_ref-united_Kingdom_Ebola_149-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-united_Kingdom_Ebola-149"
                ><span>[</span>149<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 3 September, the 29-year old Pooley was discharged from hospital
            after a making full recovery from the disease. According to his
            doctors, he no longer poses a risk to the community and is no longer
            infectious.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-150"><span>[</span>150<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="United_States">United States</span>
          </h4>
          <p>
            American aid worker Kent Brantly became infected with Ebola, while
            working in a Monrovia treatment centre as medical director for the
            aid group
            <a
              href="/wiki/Samaritan%E2%80%99s_Purse"
              title="Samaritan’s Purse"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Samaritan’s Purse</a
            >; Nancy Writebol, one of Brantly's missionary co-workers, became
            infected at the same time.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-151"><span>[</span>151<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-152"><span>[</span>152<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-153"><span>[</span>153<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Both were flown to the United States at the beginning of August for
            further treatment in
            <a
              href="/wiki/Atlanta,_Georgia"
              title="Atlanta, Georgia"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Atlanta</a
            >'s
            <a
              href="/wiki/Emory_University_Hospital"
              title="Emory University Hospital"
              >Emory University Hospital</a
            >, near the headquarters of the Centers for Disease Control.<sup
              id="cite_ref-154"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-154"><span>[</span>154<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            On 21 August, both Dr. Brantly and Ms. Writebol were discharged from
            Emory University Hospital, having recovered from the virus. Neither
            patient is considered contagious.<sup
              id="cite_ref-Yahoo:_US_discharge_155-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Yahoo:_US_discharge-155"
                ><span>[</span>155<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On Thursday 4 September a Boston physician, Dr Rick Sacra, was
            airlifted from Liberia to be treated in the United States. He is the
            third US missionary, working for
            <a href="/wiki/Serving_In_Mission" title="Serving In Mission"
              >Serving In Mission</a
            >
            (SIM), who tested positive for the disease. Dr Sacra will be treated
            in
            <a href="/wiki/Omaha" title="Omaha" class="mw-redirect">Omaha</a> at
            the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Nebraska_Medical_Center"
              title="Nebraska Medical Center"
              >Nebraska Medical Center</a
            >.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-156"><span>[</span>156<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Dr. Sacra landed on Friday 5 September at the
            <a href="/wiki/Offutt_Air_Force_Base" title="Offutt Air Force Base"
              >Offutt Air Force Base</a
            >. A heavy police guard escorted the ambulance to the hospital in
            <a href="/wiki/Nebraska" title="Nebraska">Nebraska</a>. The doctor
            did not get infected while treating Ebola patients, but was exposed
            to the virus while delivering babies at a hospital in Liberia.
            <sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-157"><span>[</span>157<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Other_countries_and_economic_regions"
              >Other countries and economic regions</span
            >
          </h3>
          <h4>
            <span
              class="mw-headline"
              id="Economic_Community_of_West_African_States"
              >Economic Community of West African States</span
            >
          </h4>
          <p>
            On 30 March, during the 44th Summit of the heads of state and
            government of West Africa,
            <a
              href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States"
              title="Economic Community of West African States"
              >Economic Community of West African States</a
            >
            (ECOWAS) disbursed US$250,000 to deal with the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-disburse_158-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-disburse-158"
                ><span>[</span>158<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            At the event in July of that year, the Nigerian government donated
            US$500,000 to the Liberian government to aid the fight against the
            virus.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-159"><span>[</span>159<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            In July, the WHO convened an emergency sub-regional meeting with
            health ministers from eleven countries in
            <a
              href="/wiki/Accra,_Ghana"
              title="Accra, Ghana"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Accra, Ghana</a
            >.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-160"><span>[</span>160<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            On 3 July, the West African states announced collaboration on a new
            strategy, and the creation of a World Health Organization
            sub-regional centre in Guinea "to co-ordinate technical
            support";<sup id="cite_ref-bbcjuly3_161-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-bbcjuly3-161"
                ><span>[</span>161<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            the centre was inaugurated in Conakry on 24 July.<sup
              id="cite_ref-162"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-162"><span>[</span>162<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 31 July, the WHO and West Africa nations announced
            $100&#160;million in aid to help contain the disease.<sup
              id="cite_ref-163"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-163"><span>[</span>163<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="European_Union">European Union</span>
          </h4>
          <p>
            In March, the
            <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission"
              >European Commission</a
            >
            (EC) gave €500,000 to help contain the spread of the virus in Guinea
            and its neighbouring countries. The EC has also sent a health expert
            to Guinea to help assess the situation and liaise with the local
            authorities. EU Commissioner for International Cooperation,
            Humanitarian Aid and Crisis Response
            <a href="/wiki/Kristalina_Georgieva" title="Kristalina Georgieva"
              >Kristalina Georgieva</a
            >
            said: "We are deeply concerned about the spread of this virulent
            disease and our support will help ensure immediate health assistance
            to those affected by it. It's vital that we act swiftly to prevent
            the outbreak from spreading, particularly to neighbouring
            countries."<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-164"><span>[</span>164<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            In April, a
            <a href="/wiki/Mobile_laboratory" title="Mobile laboratory"
              >mobile laboratory</a
            >, capable of performing the molecular diagnosis of viral pathogens
            of risk groups 3 and 4, was deployed in Guinea by the European
            Mobile Laboratory project (EMLab) as part of the WHO/GOARN outbreak
            response. Prior samples were analyzed at the Jean Mérieux BSL-4
            Laboratory in Lyon.<sup id="cite_ref-Inserm_165-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Inserm-165"
                ><span>[</span>165<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            Germany's Foreign Office issued travel warnings for all affected
            countries at the end of July,<sup
              id="cite_ref-166"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-166"><span>[</span>166<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Spain did so on 2 August<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-167"><span>[</span>167<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            and the UK did on 20 August.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-168"><span>[</span>168<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Australia">Australia</span></h4>
          <p>
            On 14 August, the Australian ambassador to the People’s Republic of
            China revealed that the Australian government would donate $US1
            million to the World Health Organisation, in addition to its annual
            support, to assist in combating the Ebola outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-169"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-169"><span>[</span>169<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Canada">Canada</span></h4>
          <p>
            On 12 August, the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Public_Health_Agency_of_Canada"
              title="Public Health Agency of Canada"
              >Public Health Agency of Canada</a
            >
            (PHAC) announced that the country would donate between 800 and 1,000
            doses of an untested vaccine (VSV-EBOV) to the World Health
            Organization.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-170"><span>[</span>170<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            The offer was made by the Minister of Health directly to the
            Director General of the WHO as part of the country's commitment to
            containment efforts. The
            <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Canada" title="Government of Canada"
              >Government of Canada</a
            >
            holds the intellectual property associated with the vaccine, but has
            licensed BioProtection Systems of
            <a href="/wiki/Ames,_Iowa" title="Ames, Iowa">Ames, Iowa</a> to
            develop the product for use in humans.<sup
              id="cite_ref-171"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-171"><span>[</span>171<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            As of 12 August, Canada's contribution to address the spread of the
            Ebola virus in West Africa is estimated at $5,195,000. This includes
            resources dedicated to humanitarian, security, and public health
            interventions.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-172"><span>[</span>172<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 26 August, the PHAC said it is preparing to bring home three
            members from their mobile laboratory in Sierra Leone. The three
            Canadians were among six workers at the mobile lab. The team is from
            the
            <a
              href="/wiki/National_Microbiology_Laboratory"
              title="National Microbiology Laboratory"
              >National Microbiology Laboratory</a
            >
            in <a href="/wiki/Winnipeg" title="Winnipeg">Winnipeg</a>. The
            recall follows the diagnoses of three persons, staying at the same
            hotel as the team members, with the Ebola virus. The team members
            had no direct contact with the infected persons and are not showing
            any signs of the disease. The team members will be monitored as they
            travel back to Canada and will remain in voluntary isolation until
            cleared, officials from the PHAC said.<sup
              id="cite_ref-173"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-173"><span>[</span>173<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 6 September, the Public Health Agency of Canada announced that
            they will be resuming work at the Kailahun mobile laboratory after
            recalling them in late August for safety reasons. A three person
            team have been sent to the laboratory in eastern Sierra Leone. The
            team will rotate on a monthly bases.<sup
              id="cite_ref-174"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-174"><span>[</span>174<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Chad">Chad</span></h4>
          <p>
            The Prime Minister of <a href="/wiki/Chad" title="Chad">Chad</a>,
            <a
              href="/wiki/Kalzeubet_Pahimi_Deubet"
              title="Kalzeubet Pahimi Deubet"
              >Kalzeubet Pahimi Deubet</a
            >, said it will follow in the footsteps of South Africa and impose
            travel restrictions to and from the countries currently affected by
            the Ebola outbreak. Chad will close all its borders to Nigeria to
            prevent the spread of the disease to the country. He added that this
            would have an economic impact to Chad and the region, but the
            restrictions are necessary.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-175"><span>[</span>175<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="China">China</span></h4>
          <p>
            A Chinese plane carrying supplies worth 30 million yuan (4.9 million
            US dollars) arrived in Guinea, Sierra Leone and Liberia on 11
            August.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-176"><span>[</span>176<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-177"><span>[</span>177<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            This is their second Ebola relief after the first batch delivered in
            May to Guinea, Liberia, Sierra Leone and Guinea-Bissau. The supplies
            include medical protective clothes, disinfectants, thermo-detectors
            and medicines. China also sent three expert teams composed of
            epidemiologists and specialists in disinfection and protection as
            well as medical supplies to Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone despite
            high risk of infection.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-178"><span>[</span>178<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-179"><span>[</span>179<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Before their arrival, eight members of a Chinese medical team sent
            to assist patients in Sierra Leone's hospitals were quarantined
            after treating Ebola patients.
          </p>
          <p>
            Some Chinese companies in West Africa also joined the relief
            efforts.
            <a href="/wiki/China_Kingho_Group" title="China Kingho Group"
              >China Kingho Group</a
            >, a leading exploration and mining company in Sierra Leone, donated
            400 million Leones (about $90,000) to the Government and People of
            Sierra Leone on 15 August.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-180"><span>[</span>180<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 16 August, Chinese President
            <a href="/wiki/Xi_Jinping" title="Xi Jinping">Xi Jinping</a> and
            <a
              href="/wiki/UN_Secretary-General"
              title="UN Secretary-General"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >UN Secretary-General</a
            >
            <a href="/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon" title="Ban Ki-moon">Ban Ki-moon</a> on
            Saturday discussed several hot issues, including Ebola, in their
            fourth meeting this year. The meeting in
            <a href="/wiki/Nanjing" title="Nanjing">Nanjing</a>, capital of east
            China's
            <a
              href="/wiki/Jiangsu_Province"
              title="Jiangsu Province"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Jiangsu Province</a
            >, was held before they attended the opening ceremony of the
            <a
              href="/wiki/2014_Summer_Youth_Olympics"
              title="2014 Summer Youth Olympics"
              >2nd Summer Youth Olympic Games</a
            >.
          </p>
          <p>
            Xi said China will continue to make joint efforts with the
            international community to prevent and control the Ebola virus
            outbreak that has hit West Africa. China has provided emergency
            medical assistance to Ebola-hit countries and sent expert groups.
            China's medical teams in the countries are working with local staff,
            according to Xi.
          </p>
          <p>
            Xi also spoke highly of the measures taken by the
            <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations"
              >United Nations</a
            >
            and
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization"
              title="World Health Organization"
              >World Health Organization</a
            >
            its professional institutions, and called for more assistance and
            input for medical and health services in African countries.<sup
              id="cite_ref-181"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-181"><span>[</span>181<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Colombia">Colombia</span></h4>
          <p>
            On 8 August, the Vice Minister of
            <a
              href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_and_Social_Protection_(Colombia)"
              title="Ministry of Health and Social Protection (Colombia)"
              >Health and Social Protection</a
            >
            of Colombia, Fernando Ruiz, assured the public that the Government
            is preparing itself to face the virus even though Colombia's given
            conditions don't give Ebola the chance to natively spread since "the
            bat species in charge of transmitting the disease nor the practice
            of eating it aren't present in Colombia."<sup
              id="cite_ref-182"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-182"><span>[</span>182<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Ruiz also stated that Colombians travelling to the affected parts of
            West Africa are being warned to take appropriate precautions.<sup
              id="cite_ref-183"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-183"><span>[</span>183<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Previously, on 5 August, the Ministry of Health and Social
            Protection issued a press release stating that "since the month of
            April the National Government has been closely following and
            monitoring the outbreak of the Ebola virus in West Africa and the
            State has decided to adopt word by word the contingency plan
            prepared by the
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization"
              title="World Health Organization"
              >World Health Organization</a
            >
            (WHO)."<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-184"><span>[</span>184<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Equatorial_Guinea"
              >Equatorial Guinea</span
            >
          </h4>
          <p>
            <a href="/wiki/Equatorial_Guinea" title="Equatorial Guinea"
              >Equatorial Guinea</a
            >
            temporarily stopped issuing
            <a
              href="/wiki/Visa_policy_of_Equatorial_Guinea"
              title="Visa policy of Equatorial Guinea"
              >visas</a
            >
            from neighbouring countries and cancelled regional flights by
            <a
              href="/wiki/Ceiba_Intercontinental_Airlines"
              title="Ceiba Intercontinental Airlines"
              >Ceiba Intercontinental Airlines</a
            >.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-185"><span>[</span>185<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ghana">Ghana</span></h4>
          <p>
            On 30 August the
            <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian" title="Ghanaian" class="mw-redirect"
              >Ghanaian</a
            >
            Presidency released a press statement, announcing the country's
            willingness to use <a href="/wiki/Accra" title="Accra">Accra</a> as
            a support base to help fight Ebola in the stricken countries. This
            agreement follows a telephonic meeting with the
            <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations"
              >United Nations</a
            >
            chief,
            <a href="/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon" title="Ban Ki-moon">Ban Ki-moon</a> and
            <a href="/wiki/John_Dramani_Mahama" title="John Dramani Mahama"
              >John Dramani Mahama</a
            >, the President of Ghana. Accra will serve as a base for air
            lifting medical and other supplies to countries affected by the
            Ebola outbreak, as well as personnel to curtail the disease.<sup
              id="cite_ref-186"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-186"><span>[</span>186<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="India_2">India</span></h4>
          <p>
            On 8 August, India placed all of its airports on high alert and
            stepped up surveillance of all travellers entering the country from
            Ebola-affected regions. The Union Health Minister,
            <a
              href="/wiki/Harsh_Vardhan_(Delhi_politician)"
              title="Harsh Vardhan (Delhi politician)"
              >Harsh Vardhan</a
            >, issued a statement, "There is no cause for panic. We have put in
            operation the most advanced surveillance and tracking systems." From
            9 August, passengers coming from Ebola-affected countries will have
            to complete a form before landing; the form has a check-list for
            symptoms and asks travellers from West Africa for information about
            places visited, length of stay and other important information.
          </p>
          <p>
            "The form is ready and will be officially released by Saturday. We
            will request all airlines to direct their staff to distribute the
            form in-flight, like immigration forms are given before arrival,"
            said Jagdish Prasad, director general of health services, Union
            Ministry of Health. In New Delhi, Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital in New
            Delhi has been designated as a treatment centre for Ebola Virus
            Disease (EVD) cases. A 24-hour emergency helpline will also be
            functional from Saturday. Its numbers are (011)-23061469, 3205 and
            1302. The estimated 47,000 Indians in the affected countries are
            being contacted by area diplomatic missions and supplied with
            educational material about the disease.<sup
              id="cite_ref-187"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-187"><span>[</span>187<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ivory_Coast">Ivory Coast</span></h4>
          <p>
            The
            <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast" title="Ivory Coast">Ivory Coast</a> on
            Friday 22 August released a statement on state-owned television
            announcing the closure of its borders to the neighbouring countries
            affected by the Ebola outbreak. Attempting to prevent the Ebola
            outbreak of the virus from spreading to the
            <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast" title="Ivory Coast">Ivory Coast</a> the
            government announced the closure of all its land based borders to
            the country's West African neighbours Guinea and Liberia.<sup
              id="cite_ref-188"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-188"><span>[</span>188<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            The Ivory Coast previously placed a ban on all flights to and from
            Sierra Leone, Liberia and Guinea.<sup
              id="cite_ref-Ivory_Coast_flight_ban_189-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Ivory_Coast_flight_ban-189"
                ><span>[</span>189<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Japan">Japan</span></h4>
          <p>
            In April, the Government of
            <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a> gave $520,000 through
            <a
              href="/wiki/United_Nations_Children%E2%80%99s_Fund"
              title="United Nations Children’s Fund"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >the United Nations Children’s Fund</a
            >
            (UNICEF) to support the Ebola outbreak response in Guinea.<sup
              id="cite_ref-190"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-190"><span>[</span>190<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            In August, another $1.5 million in additional support was provided
            to be disbursed via the WHO, UNICEF and
            <a href="/wiki/Red_Cross" title="Red Cross" class="mw-redirect"
              >Red Cross</a
            >, and will be used for measures to prevent Ebola infections and to
            provide medical supplies.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-191"><span>[</span>191<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 25 August, Japanese authorities announced that they would be
            willing to provide access to an anti-influenza drug currently under
            development called
            <a href="/wiki/Favipiravir" title="Favipiravir">favipiravir</a> to
            try to treat EVD patients.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-192"><span>[</span>192<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Fujifilm Holdings Corp and MediVector have reportedly approached the
            U.S.
            <a
              href="/wiki/Food_and_Drug_Administration"
              title="Food and Drug Administration"
              >Food and Drug Administration</a
            >
            to request approval for this experimental use of favipiravir. Up to
            20,000 doses of favipiravir would currently be available.
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Kenya">Kenya</span></h4>
          <p>
            The Kenyan government banned people travelling from or through
            Sierra Leone, Guinea and Liberia for all ports of entry.<sup
              id="cite_ref-193"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-193"><span>[</span>193<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Morocco">Morocco</span></h4>
          <p>
            Beginning in April,
            <a href="/wiki/Morocco" title="Morocco">Morocco</a> reinforced
            medical surveillance at the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Mohammed_V_International_Airport"
              title="Mohammed V International Airport"
              >Casablanca airport</a
            >, a regional hub for flights from and to West Africa.<sup
              id="cite_ref-194"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-194"><span>[</span>194<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-195"><span>[</span>195<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            In early August, Liberian interior minister Morris Dukuly announced
            the Ebola death of a Liberian man in the country,<sup
              id="cite_ref-heritage_196-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-heritage-196"
                ><span>[</span>196<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            but the Moroccan Ministry of Health announced that the person died
            of a heart attack, rather than Ebola.<sup
              id="cite_ref-197"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-197"><span>[</span>197<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Philippines">Philippines</span></h4>
          <p>
            The Philippine
            <a
              href="/wiki/Department_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Philippines)"
              title="Department of Foreign Affairs (Philippines)"
              >Department of Foreign Affairs</a
            >
            has raised Alert Level 2 in Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone and has
            temporarily halted the sending of Filipino workers to the affected
            countries since 30 June. Filipino seafarers are also cautioned about
            potentially contracting Ebola when their ship docks in affected
            countries.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-198"><span>[</span>198<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            The
            <a
              href="/wiki/Department_of_Health_(Philippines)"
              title="Department of Health (Philippines)"
              >Department of Health</a
            >
            expressed its willingness to send medical workers to Ebola-affected
            countries to help contain the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-199"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-199"><span>[</span>199<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            On 23 August, the Philippines announced that it is pulling out its
            115
            <a
              href="/wiki/UN_peacekeepers"
              title="UN peacekeepers"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >UN peacekeepers</a
            >
            stationed in Liberia due to the increasing health risk the troops
            face due to the outbreak.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-200"><span>[</span>200<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Qatar">Qatar</span></h4>
          <p>
            Qatar has banned the import of live animals, food and meat products
            from Guinea, Liberia, Sierra Leone and Nigeria as a precaution
            against Ebola.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-201"><span>[</span>201<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Seychelles">Seychelles</span></h4>
          <p>
            <a href="/wiki/Seychelles" title="Seychelles">Seychelles</a>
            introduced a visa requirement for the citizens of Sierra Leone,
            Liberia, Guinea-Bissau, Guinea Conakry, Nigeria, Cameroon, Chad,
            Niger, Burkina Faso, Mali, Benin, Ivory Coast, Ghana, Togo, Congo,
            D.R. Congo, Gambia, Mauritania, and Senegal. Citizens of these
            countries will require a visa until the Ebola outbreak is declared
            over.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-202"><span>[</span>202<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Members of the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Sierra_Leone_national_football_team"
              title="Sierra Leone national football team"
              >Sierra Leone national football team</a
            >
            were refused visas over the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-203"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-203"><span>[</span>203<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Saudi_Arabia">Saudi Arabia</span>
          </h4>
          <p>
            On 1 April,
            <a href="/wiki/Saudi_Arabia" title="Saudi Arabia">Saudi Arabia</a>
            stopped issuing visas for the
            <a href="/wiki/Muslim" title="Muslim">Muslim</a> pilgrimage to
            <a href="/wiki/Mecca" title="Mecca">Mecca</a> to people from Guinea,
            Liberia, and Sierra Leone.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-204"><span>[</span>204<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 5 August Saudi Arabia announced that it would block issuance of
            <a href="/wiki/Hajj" title="Hajj">Hajj</a> and
            <a href="/wiki/Umrah" title="Umrah">Umrah</a> visas to the citizens
            of Sierra Leone, Guinea and Liberia.<sup
              id="cite_ref-205"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-205"><span>[</span>205<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 6 August, the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_(Saudi_Arabia)"
              title="Ministry of Health (Saudi Arabia)"
              >Saudi Ministry of Health</a
            >
            advised citizens and residents of Saudi Arabia to avoid travelling
            to Liberia, Sierra Leone, and Guinea until further notice.<sup
              id="cite_ref-206"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-206"><span>[</span>206<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="South_Africa">South Africa</span>
          </h4>
          <p>
            On 21 August
            <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a>
            announced a ban on all travelers from the three Ebola-hit West
            African nations. A government spokesman confirmed they are following
            other countries responses to the disease outbreak. The health
            ministry of South Africa confirmed that the country's citizens would
            be asked to limit travel to absolutely essential needs, if going to
            the countries involved in the current outbreak. All South Africans
            returning from these countries would only be allowed back after
            undergoing extensive medical tests, and quarantine, if
            necessary.<sup
              id="cite_ref-wsj-sa-travel-ban_207-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-wsj-sa-travel-ban-207"
                ><span>[</span>207<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Sri_Lanka">Sri Lanka</span></h4>
          <p>
            <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka">Sri Lanka</a> cancelled
            visa on-arrival facility on 21 August for citizens of Guinea,
            Liberia, Nigeria and Sierra Leone.<sup
              id="cite_ref-208"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-208"><span>[</span>208<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h4>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="United_Kingdom_2"
              >United Kingdom</span
            >
          </h4>
          <p>
            The UK government has made £2 million available to partners
            including the International Federation of the Red Cross (IFRC) and
            Médecins Sans Frontières that are operating in Sierra Leone and
            Liberia to tackle the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-209"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-209"><span>[</span>209<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Additionally a £6.5 million rapid response research initiative has
            been announced jointly by the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Department_for_International_Development"
              title="Department for International Development"
              >Department for International Development</a
            >
            and the
            <a href="/wiki/Wellcome_Trust" title="Wellcome Trust"
              >Wellcome Trust</a
            >
            to better inform the management of Ebola outbreaks. This includes
            research which could help tackle the current outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-210"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-210"><span>[</span>210<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 26 August,
            <a href="/wiki/British_Airways" title="British Airways"
              >British Airways</a
            >
            extended its ban on flights to Liberia and Sierra Leone until 31
            December due to the declining public health situation.<sup
              id="cite_ref-211"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-211"><span>[</span>211<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-212"><span>[</span>212<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            The
            <a
              href="/wiki/Foreign_Office"
              title="Foreign Office"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Foreign Office</a
            >
            issued updated travel advice in the week ending 24 August urging
            Britons to evaluate the need to travel to Sierra Leone, Guinea and
            Liberia.<sup
              id="cite_ref-united_Kingdom_Ebola_149-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-united_Kingdom_Ebola-149"
                ><span>[</span>149<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h2>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Response_by_organisations"
              >Response by organisations</span
            >
          </h2>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="World_Health_Organization"
              >World Health Organization</span
            >
          </h3>
          <p>
            The
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Health_Organisation"
              title="World Health Organisation"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >World Health Organisation</a
            >'s (WHO) Regional Director for Africa, Luis Sambo, visited the
            affected countries from 21 to 25 July, meeting with political
            leaders, ministers of health,
            <a href="/wiki/NGO" title="NGO" class="mw-redirect">NGOs</a>, and
            other agencies. He stressed the need to "promote behavioural change
            while respecting cultural practices."<sup
              id="cite_ref-WHO_25_July_2014_29-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO_25_July_2014-29"
                ><span>[</span>29<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 24 July, WHO's Director General met with agencies and donors in
            Geneva to facilitate an increase in funding and manpower to respond
            to the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-WHO_25_July_2014_29-2"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO_25_July_2014-29"
                ><span>[</span>29<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            WHO declared the outbreak an international public health emergency
            on 8 August, after a two-day teleconference of experts.<sup
              id="cite_ref-WHO_PHEIC_8Aug2014_9-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO_PHEIC_8Aug2014-9"
                ><span>[</span>9<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 11 August, they emphasised lack of supplies and capacity as one
            of the problems, while local awareness of the disease had
            increased.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-213"><span>[</span>213<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Revised guidelines on how to prevent the spread of the disease were
            released, updating guidelines from 2008.<sup
              id="cite_ref-214"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-214"><span>[</span>214<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 28 August, the WHO said it is seeking $490 million in funding to
            fight the outbreak.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-215"><span>[</span>215<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            They report that they "are on the ground establishing Ebola
            treatment centres and strengthening capacity for laboratory testing,
            contact tracing, social mobilization, safe burials, and non-Ebola
            health care" and "continue to monitor for reports of rumoured or
            suspected cases from countries around the world." Other than cases
            where individuals are suspected or have been confirmed of being
            infected with Ebola, or have had contact with cases of Ebola, the
            WHO does not recommend any travel or trade restrictions.<sup
              id="cite_ref-WHO_13-5"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-WHO-13"
                ><span>[</span>13<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="US_Centers_for_Disease_Control"
              >US Centers for Disease Control</span
            >
          </h3>
          <p>
            On 31 July, US health officials from the US
            <a
              href="/wiki/Centers_for_Disease_Control"
              title="Centers for Disease Control"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Centers for Disease Control</a
            >
            (CDC) issued a travel advisory for Guinea, Liberia, and Sierra
            Leone, warning against non-essential travel.<sup
              id="cite_ref-216"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-216"><span>[</span>216<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            By 26 August, the CDC had issued a Level 3 travel warning for Sierra
            Leone, Guinea, and Liberia and a Level 2 travel warning for
            Nigeria.<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-217"><span>[</span>217<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            The Level 3 warning is the highest that can be issued and will be in
            place until 27 February 2015. It means that United States residents
            must avoid nonessential travel to the three countries worst hit by
            the virus.
          </p>
          <p>
            By the beginning of August, the US Centers for Disease Control had
            placed staff in Guinea, Sierra Leone, Liberia, and Nigeria to assist
            the local Ministries of Health and WHO-led response to the
            outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-2014_Ebola_Outbreak_in_West_Africa_128-2"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-2014_Ebola_Outbreak_in_West_Africa-128"
                ><span>[</span>128<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            On 6 August, the Centers for Disease Control moved its Ebola
            response to Level 1 (the highest on a scale from 1 to 6) to increase
            the agency's ability to respond to the outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-yahoo1_218-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-yahoo1-218"
                ><span>[</span>218<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 29 August the CDC issued a Level 2 travel warning for the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo"
              title="Democratic Republic of the Congo"
              >Democratic Republic of the Congo</a
            >
            (DRC), warning against contact with body fluids of people with
            Ebola.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-219"><span>[</span>219<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="M.C3.A9decins_Sans_Fronti.C3.A8res"
              >Médecins Sans Frontières</span
            >
          </h3>
          <p>
            The
            <a href="/wiki/Humanitarian_aid" title="Humanitarian aid"
              >humanitarian aid</a
            >
            organisation
            <a
              href="/wiki/M%C3%A9decins_Sans_Fronti%C3%A8res"
              title="Médecins Sans Frontières"
              >Médecins Sans Frontières</a
            >
            (<i>Doctors Without Borders</i>) started its Ebola intervention in
            West Africa in March 2014 and is now present in Guinea, Liberia,
            Nigeria, and Sierra Leone. At the end of August, the organization
            runs five Ebola case management centers with a total capacity of 415
            beds. Since March, MSF has admitted a total of 1,885 patients. 907
            of these patients tested positive for Ebola, and 170 have recovered.
            MSF has deployed 184 international staff to the region and employs
            1,800 nationally hired personnel.<sup
              id="cite_ref-doctorswithoutborders.org_66-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-doctorswithoutborders.org-66"
                ><span>[</span>66<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            On 29 August MSF described the international response as slow and
            derisory.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-220"><span>[</span>220<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Samaritan.27s_Purse"
              >Samaritan's Purse</span
            >
          </h3>
          <p>
            <a href="/wiki/Samaritan%27s_Purse" title="Samaritan's Purse"
              >Samaritan's Purse</a
            >
            is also providing direct patient care in multiple locations in
            Liberia.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-221"><span>[</span>221<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            At a congressional committee hearing on 7 August 2014, the head of
            Samaritan's Purse stated that "The disease is uncontained and out of
            control in West Africa."<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-222"><span>[</span>222<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="World_Food_Program"
              >World Food Program</span
            >
          </h3>
          <p>
            On 18 August,
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Food_Program"
              title="World Food Program"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >World Food Program</a
            >
            announced plans to mobilise food assistance for an estimated 1
            million people living in restricted access areas.<sup
              id="cite_ref-223"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-223"><span>[</span>223<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h3>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="World_Bank_Group"
              >World Bank Group</span
            >
          </h3>
          <p>
            The
            <a href="/wiki/World_Bank_Group" title="World Bank Group"
              >World Bank Group</a
            >
            has pledged up to US $200 million in emergency funding to help
            Guinea, Liberia, and Sierra Leone contain the spread of Ebola
            infections, help their communities cope with the economic impact of
            the crisis, and improve public health systems throughout West
            Africa.
            <sup id="cite_ref-worldbank1_89-1" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-worldbank1-89"
                ><span>[</span>89<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Virology">Virology</span></h2>
          <div class="thumb tright">
            <div class="thumbinner" style="width: 222px">
              <a
                href="/wiki/File:Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg"
                class="image"
                ><img
                  alt=""
                  src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg/220px-Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg"
                  width="220"
                  height="146"
                  class="thumbimage"
                  srcset="
                    //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg/330px-Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg 1.5x,
                    //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/97/Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg                                               2x
                  "
                  data-file-width="400"
                  data-file-height="266"
              /></a>
              <div class="thumbcaption">
                <div class="magnify">
                  <a
                    href="/wiki/File:Biosafety_level_4_hazmat_suit.jpg"
                    class="internal"
                    title="Enlarge"
                  ></a>
                </div>
                A researcher working with the Ebola virus while wearing a
                <a
                  href="/wiki/Positive_pressure_personnel_suit"
                  title="Positive pressure personnel suit"
                  >BSL-4 positive pressure suit</a
                >
                to avoid infection
              </div>
            </div>
          </div>
          <div class="hatnote relarticle mainarticle">
            Main article:
            <a href="/wiki/Ebola_virus" title="Ebola virus">Ebola virus</a>
          </div>
          <p>
            The strain of virus affecting people in the current outbreak is a
            member of the Zaire lineage.<sup
              id="cite_ref-plosx_224-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-plosx-224"
                ><span>[</span>224<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            An article published in the
            <a
              href="/wiki/New_England_Journal_of_Medicine"
              title="New England Journal of Medicine"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >New England Journal of Medicine</a
            >
            on-line in April 2014 asserted that while the
            <a href="/wiki/Ebola_virus" title="Ebola virus">Zaire ebolavirus</a>
            (EBOV) in Guinea shared 97% of its genetic code with the Zaïre
            lineage, it was of a different
            <a href="/wiki/Clade" title="Clade">clade</a> than the strains from
            outbreaks in the
            <a
              href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_Congo"
              title="Democratic Republic of Congo"
              class="mw-redirect"
              >Democratic Republic of Congo</a
            >
            and <a href="/wiki/Gabon" title="Gabon">Gabon</a>, and constituted a
            new strain indigenous to Guinea, and was not imported from Central
            Africa to West Africa.<sup
              id="cite_ref-Baize-2014_14-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Baize-2014-14"
                ><span>[</span>14<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            This result, however was contradicted by two subsequent reports.
          </p>
          <p>
            The first of these reports reached the conclusion that the outbreak
            "is likely caused by a Zaire ebolavirus lineage that has spread from
            Central Africa into Guinea and West Africa in recent decades, and
            does not represent the emergence of a divergent and
            <a
              href="/wiki/Endemic_(epidemiology)"
              title="Endemic (epidemiology)"
              >endemic</a
            >
            virus."<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated4_225-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated4-225"
                ><span>[</span>225<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            A second report published in June 2014 also supports the latter
            view, determining that it was "extremely unlikely that this virus
            falls outside the genetic diversity of the Zaïre lineage" and that
            their analysis "unambiguously supports Guinea 2014 EBOV as a member
            of the Zaïre lineage."<sup
              id="cite_ref-plosx_224-1"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-plosx-224"
                ><span>[</span>224<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            Scientists from the
            <a href="/wiki/Broad_Institute" title="Broad Institute"
              >Broad Institute</a
            >
            and
            <a href="/wiki/Harvard_University" title="Harvard University"
              >Harvard University</a
            >, in partnership with the Sierra Leone Ministry of Health and
            Sanitation, may have uncovered clues that set this Ebola outbreak
            apart from previous outbreaks. For this study, 99 Ebola virus
            <a href="/wiki/Genomes" title="Genomes" class="mw-redirect"
              >genomes</a
            >
            were collected and
            <a href="/wiki/Sequenced" title="Sequenced" class="mw-redirect"
              >sequenced</a
            >
            from 78 patients diagnosed with the Ebola virus during the first 24
            days of the outbreak in Sierra Leone. The team found more than 300
            genetic changes that make the 2014 Ebola virus distinct from
            previous outbreaks. It is still unclear whether these differences
            are related to the severity of the current outbreak.<sup
              id="cite_ref-226"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-226"><span>[</span>226<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-Gire_2014_227-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Gire_2014-227"
                ><span>[</span>227<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            Five members of the research team became ill and died from Ebola
            before the study was published in August.<sup
              id="cite_ref-Science_228-0"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Science-228"
                ><span>[</span>228<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-Gire_2014_227-1" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-Gire_2014-227"
                ><span>[</span>227<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-229"><span>[</span>229<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h2>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Popular_culture"
              >Popular culture</span
            >
          </h2>
          <p>
            "<a href="/wiki/Ebola_in_Town" title="Ebola in Town"
              >Ebola in Town</a
            >," a
            <a href="/wiki/Dance_tune" title="Dance tune" class="mw-redirect"
              >dance tune</a
            >
            by a group of West African rappers warning people of the dangers of
            the Ebola virus and explaining how to react, became popular in
            Guinea and Liberia during the first quarter of 2014.<sup
              id="cite_ref-230"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-230"><span>[</span>230<span>]</span></a></sup
            ><sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-231"><span>[</span>231<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            In August 2014,
            <a href="/wiki/George_Weah" title="George Weah">George Weah</a> and
            Ghanaian musician Sidney produced a song to raise awareness about
            Ebola.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-232"><span>[</span>232<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            There are a number of Ebola-themed jokes circulating in West
            Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-233" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-233"><span>[</span>233<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <h2>
            <span class="mw-headline" id="Timeline_of_the_outbreak"
              >Timeline of the outbreak</span
            >
          </h2>
          <p>
            A timeline of the outbreak follows, using data reported by the
            Centers for Disease Control and Prevention<sup
              id="cite_ref-234"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-234"><span>[</span>234<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            and the
            <a
              href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization"
              title="World Health Organization"
              >World Health Organization</a
            >
            (WHO).<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated2_235-0" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2-235"
                ><span>[</span>235<span>]</span></a
              ></sup
            >
            The table also includes suspected cases that have yet to be
            confirmed for the virus. The reports are sourced from official
            information from the affected countries' health ministries. WHO has
            stated the reported numbers "vastly underestimate the magnitude of
            the outbreak".<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-236"><span>[</span>236<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
            Cases in remote areas may also be missed.<sup
              id="cite_ref-237"
              class="reference"
              ><a href="#cite_note-237"><span>[</span>237<span>]</span></a></sup
            >
          </p>
          <p>
            Note that numbers for cases and deaths are in constant flux. Numbers
            reported for cases may include probable or suspected cases; numbers
            are revised downward if a suspected case turns out to be negative.
          </p>
